                            2. A.    11.21.17 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A. CAPE (Citizens and Parents for Education) Steering Committee 
     for TSD Building & Site Sinking Fund 
As you no doubt have heard, voters overwhelmingly came out in favor 
of the Troy School District one-mill Building & Site Sinking Fund  in the 
November 7 th Election. The proposal passed with about 68 % of the 
votes, which is a testament to the support of our parents and the Troy 
Community. The results exemplified their faith in our schools and the 
value they see in the exemplary education we provide for the students 
of this District. We are very grateful for their c onfidence in the fiscal 
responsibility of our Board of Education and their trust in our 
stewardship of their investment. 
Passage of the Sinking Fund could not have come without the tireless 
work of many people  — parents, community volunteers, staff and 
administrators. Tonight, we would like to personally thank the driving 
force behind the TSD Building & Site Sinking Fund —the Steering 
Committee of CAPE ( Citizens and Parents for Education ), who 
volunteered countless hours of their time to ensure that this initiative 
passed. Their dedication and hard work—both behind the scenes and in 
public forums—was integral to the success of the Sinking Fund Proposal. 
We would like to especially acknowledge CAPE chair Tracy Heikka, who 
coordinated the group’s efforts and  worked tirelessly to ensure this 
important measure passed.  
Mrs. Heikka would say it took a team, so t onight, we are pleased to 
recognize the CAPE Steering Committee for their exemplary service  to 
the Troy School District.  
Please come up as we read your name and then we would like Mrs. 
Heikka to come up at the end and say a few words, if she wishes. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Read names and pass out certificates.

BUSINESS 
2016-2017 Audit Report David  Youngstrom,  Audit  Partner  from  Yeo  & Yeo, 
provided a presentation to the Board of Education on 
the audit of the 2016-2017 fiscal year. A draft copy of 
the District’s Audit Report was presented in detail to the 
Board at the special workshop earlier this evening. Mr. 
Youngstrom reviewed governmental revenue and 
expenditures, general fund revenue and expenditures, 
instructional expenditures comparison to Oakland 
County Schools, the general and historical fund 
balance,  and  the  history  of  the  fund  balance  as a 
4 October17, 2017  
11
percentage of expenditures. He reviewed internal 
control matters, material weaknesses or deficiencies, 
compliance findings, and future challenges. Some of 
the challenges he mentione d are the cost of fringe 
benefits, state economic/political conditions, retirement 
rate increases, the uncer tainty of state revenue 
sources, special education costs, the unfunded pension 
liability and the new accounting standards. 
Mr. Hauff asked how much time is put into conducting 
the audit. Mr. Youngstrom shared that 3–4 auditors are 
onsite for three weeks and spend approximately 3-400 
hours working on the audit. Mr. Youngstrom thanked 
Mrs. Trotter, Mr. West, and their staff for their effort 
and for being prepared. Mr. Schmidt wanted to clarify 
that the fund balance is more of a savings account and 
used as needed before funds are received from the 
government. 
2016-2017 Audit Report 
Board of Education accept and approve the Troy School 
District’s audited 2017 financial statements as 
presented by Yeo & Yeo.

Invitations were sent to eight potential bidders  with seven responding. After analyzing the 
information provided in all of the proposals, the lowest qualified bidder to supply copy paper 
for the 2017-18 school year was Paper Express, Inc.   
It is therefore recommended that Paper Express, Inc. be awarded bid package 9847 – Copy 
Paper 201 7-18 School Year  in the amount of $ 88,830.00. Attached is copy of the bid 
tabulation.

 Yes: 
 No: 
138
Memorandum 
TO:         Board of Education 
FROM:   Beth Soggs 
RE:          PowerSchool Implementation 
Date:      November 14, 2017 
eSchools is the system Troy currently uses for its student management system, and it was recently 
acquired by PowerSchool.  PowerSchool has an SMS package that is widely used across the State of 
Michigan representing over 735,000 students/455 districts.  All districts in Macomb County utilize 
PowerSchool, as well as four districts in Oakland County (Birmingham, Lake Orion, Oxford, and West 
Bloomfield).  In comparison, eSchools currently represents 42,000 students/11 districts in Michigan.  We 
are planning to migrate to PowerSchool for several reasons including: 
1. Staff/Parent concerns with eSchools 
2. Ability to collaborate with neighboring districts on reports, training, integrations, etc. 
3. On-Line registration and forms 
4. Single sign-on for parents to access student information items such as Schoology, payments, 
attendance, report cards, etc. 
5. Improved Parent/Teacher Portals (HAC/TAC) 
Demonstrations have been held for administrative staff and the staff responsible for secondary 
scheduling.  The feedback from those that attended the demonstrations has been positive.   
Timeline 
It is planned to have the initial set-up complete in early January, so that tasks that occur second 
semester in preparation for the upcoming school year can be built in the new system (i.e. kindergarten 
round-up, secondary scheduling, elementary scheduling, etc.).   
Teacher gradebook training will take place for tech chairs in advance so a train the trainer model can be 
used to get all teachers training as they return for the new year.  
Administrative training will be scheduled throughout the second semester of the 2017/18 school year 
and during the summer with a plan to deliver training on specific functions as the need to complete a 
specific function occurs.  This will ensure that staff are able to implement and practice the new 
tasks/processes learned.  
For the start of the 2018/19 school year, Troy will be completely live on PowerSchool. Communication 
will be made with parents in a timely manner so that they can set up accounts in the parent portal and 
teachers will have training available as they return for the start of the school year.  
139
Pricing 
The implementation proposal is attached for your review.  The Services costs are one-time 
implementation, training, and set up fees.    The increase in annual software licenses is $27,348 when 
compared to the current pricing for eSchools.  The majority of this increase is attributed to the purchase 
of the on-line registration module which will streamline the enrollment process and simplify annual 
form completion for parents.   
As with any new product implementation, some processes may change in order to meet the constraints 
of the system, but in the end, we feel that PowerSchool will provide a better user experience for our 
staff, students, and parents. 
Please let us know if you have any questions.   
140

School Bus Replacement Program - 2016 Bond Series 2 - 2017-18 School Year  
Fifteen of the District’s bus fleet have reached their end -of-life cycle. Michigan School 
Business Officials, MSBO, offers a cooperative bus purchasing service to obtain competitive 
pricing from bidding dealers.  
This service was utilized to obtain school bus pricing for several different models that match 
our District’s bus needs. After analyzing the information provided from three proposals and 
reviewing these proposals  with Dominick Asaro, Transportation Liaison, collaboratively we 
recommend the low bidder, Hoekstra Transportation.

in the amount of $1,448,346.00, as detailed in the attached bid tabulation.

Come a day early and attend the Equity Symposium!
Join hundreds of public education advocates on Capitol Hill and help shape the decisions made in 
Washington D.C. that directly impact our students. At the 2018 Advocacy Institute, you’ll gain insight into 
the most critical issues affecting public education, sharpen your advocacy skills, and prepare for effective 
meetings with your representatives.
Whether you are an expert advocator or a novice, attend and experience inspirational keynote speakers 
and education sessions featuring policymakers, legal experts and policy influencers. All designed to help 
you advocate for your students and communities.
Six Reasons to Attend NSBA’s 2018 Advocacy Institute:
1. Champion the interests of our students and pu blic schools with your Members of Congress.
2. Network with your fellow school board members to share best practices  to engage policy makers and 
impact policymaking.
3. Enhance your ability to tell your story to influence the national education debate.

Advocacy Institute Speakers
Cokie Roberts
February 5, 2018
Political Commentator
Cokie Roberts is a political commentator for ABC News and NPR. In 
her more than forty years in broadcasting, she has won countless 
awards, including three Emmys. She has been inducted into the 
Broadcasting and Cable Hall of Fame, and was cited by the American 
Women in Radio and Television as one of the fifty greatest women in 
the history of broadcasting. In addition to her reporting, Roberts has 
written six New York Times bestsellers, most dealing with the roles of 
women in U.S. history.
In addition to her appearances on the airwaves, Roberts, along with her husband, Steven V. Roberts, writes 
a weekly column syndicated in newspapers around the country by Universal U Click. The Roberts also 
wrote two books together: Our Haggadah: Uniting Traditions for Interfaith Families, published in 2011 and 
From this Day Forward, an account of their now almost fifty year marriage and other marriages in American 
history. The book immediately went onto The New York Times bestseller list, following Cokie Roberts’s 
number one bestseller, We Are Our Mothers’ Daughters, an account of American women’s roles and 
relationships over time. Roberts’s other books: Founding Mothers, published in 2004 and Ladies of Liberty in 
2008, along with her recently published Capital Dames, about women and Washington in the Civil War, all 
also rated as New York Times bestsellers. Roberts' latest release is a lavishly illustrated children's book, 
Ladies of Liberty: The Women Who Shaped Our Nation.
151
Page 1 of 2Advocacy Institute Speakers | National School Boards Association
11/16/2017https://www.nsba.org/events/advocacy-institute/advocacy-institute-speakers
Roberts holds more than twenty-five honorary degrees, serves on the boards of several nonprofit 
institutions and President Bush appointed her to his Commission on Service and Civic Participation. In 
2008 the Library of Congress named her a “Living Legend,” one of the very few Americans to have attained 
that honor.
